s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.78151pt{\gamma}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-8.76802pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-3.96484pt{\gamma}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-7.95135pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
,
fix a bijection
f_{s}\colon\lambda\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.98872pt{\alpha}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.88469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.98872pt{\alpha}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.88469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.2084pt{\alpha}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-10.1341pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.39174pt{\alpha}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-9.31743pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\times J\times\{s\}
and set
B_{s\conc\beta}=P_{f_{s}(\beta)}
for all
\beta<\lambda
.
We know check that all of
(i)
–
(v)
are satisfied.
Condition
(i)
is obvious, while condition
(ii)
follows from the fact that
\{B_{t}\mid\operatorname{lh}(t)=\alpha\}=\mathcal{P}
.
Condition
(iii)
holds by construction, as it is enough to let
t_{s\conc\beta}
be the first coordinate of
f_{s}(\beta)
, for every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.78151pt{\gamma}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-8.76802pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-3.96484pt{\gamma}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-7.95135pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
and
\beta<\lambda
.
We now check
(iv)
.
If
\xi=\xi^{\prime}+1>1
is a successor ordinal, then by inductive hypothesis
B_{s}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi^{\prime}}
for every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.78151pt{\gamma}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-8.76802pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-3.96484pt{\gamma}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-7.95135pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, and since
C^{(j)}_{\gamma}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\bar{\xi}}
and
\bar{\xi}\leq\xi^{\prime}
, we conclude
B_{s\conc\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi^{\prime}}
.
If
\xi
is a limit ordinal of cofinality
\lambda
, set
\nu(\alpha)=\max\{\nu(\gamma),\bar{\xi}\}<\xi
. By inductive hypothesis again,
B_{s}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\gamma)}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\alpha)}
for every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.78151pt{\gamma}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-8.76802pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-3.96484pt{\gamma}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-7.95135pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, and
C^{(j)}_{\gamma}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\bar{\xi}}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\alpha)}
: hence
B_{s\conc\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\alpha)}
.
If instead
\xi
is a limit ordinal of cofinality smaller than
\lambda
, then by inductive hypothesis
B_{s}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}
and
C^{(j)}_{\gamma}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\bar{\xi}}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}
, hence
B_{s\conc\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}
.
Finally, using that
\mathcal{P}
is a disjoint covering of
X
, it is easy to verify that
(v)
holds by taking
K=\{s\conc\beta\mid s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.39105pt{\gamma}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-12.97215pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.78151pt{\gamma}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-8.76802pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-3.96484pt{\gamma}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-7.95135pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\wedge B_{s\conc\beta}\subseteq C^{(0)}_{\gamma}\}
.

Now let
\alpha>0
be a limit ordinal.
For every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.98872pt{\alpha}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.88469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.98872pt{\alpha}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.88469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.2084pt{\alpha}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-10.1341pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.39174pt{\alpha}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-9.31743pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, set
B_{s}=\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha}B_{s\restriction\beta}
.
Then
(i)
is satisfied by construction, while
(ii)
holds because by inductive hypothesis it is satisfied at all levels
\beta<\alpha
. Condition
(iii)
is satisfied by setting
t_{s}=\bigcup_{\beta<\alpha}t_{s\restriction\beta}
.
To prove
(iv)
we again consider various cases.
If
\xi=\xi^{\prime}+1
is a successor ordinal, then by inductive hypothesis
B_{s\restriction\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi^{\prime}}
for every
\beta<\alpha
, hence
B_{s}=\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha}B_{s\restriction\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi^{\prime}}
as well.
If
\xi
is a limit ordinal of cofinality
\lambda
, then we set
\nu(\alpha)=\sup_{\beta<\alpha}\nu(\beta)
: since
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)=\lambda
, we still have
\nu(\alpha)<\xi
. Then by inductive hypothesis
B_{s\restriction\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\beta)}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\alpha)}
, hence
B_{s}=\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha}B_{s\restriction\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\alpha)}
too. Finally, if
\xi
is a limit ordinal with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
, then
B_{s\restriction\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}
for every
\beta<\alpha
, and thus
B_{s}=\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha}B_{s\restriction\beta}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}
as well.
Condition
(v)
is relevant only at successor stages, and hence our proof is complete.
∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
\lambda
be regular,
(X,\tau)\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
.
Given a family
(A_{i})_{i<\lambda}
of
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(\tau)
subsets of
X
, there is a topology
\tau^{\prime}
on X such that:

(1)

(X,\tau^{\prime})\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
;

(2)

\tau\subseteq\tau^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(\tau)
if
\xi
is either a successor ordinal or a limit ordinal with cofinality
\lambda
, and
\tau\subseteq\tau^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}(\tau)
otherwise;

(3)

A_{i}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{1}(\tau^{\prime})
for every
i<\lambda
.

Proof.

Let
h\colon D\to X
be as in Lemma
6.1
. Then
it is enough to let
\tau^{\prime}
be the pushforward via
h
of the topology induced by
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
on
D
.
∎

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\lambda
be regular,
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and suppose that
Y
is spherically complete. Let
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
be either a successor ordinal or a limit ordinal of cofinality
\lambda
.
Then

\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)=\lambda\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y).

If
\xi
is limit, then we further have

\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)=\lambda\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y).

Proof.

For the right-to-left inclusions, consider
f=\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
with
f_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
or
f_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
, depending on whether
\xi
is successor or limit. Fix a clopen basis
(U_{i})_{i<\lambda}
for
Y
: it is enough to show that
f^{-1}(U_{i})\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}(X)
for every
i<\lambda
. As
U_{i}
is clopen, we have

f^{-1}(U_{i})=\bigcup_{\bar{\alpha}<\lambda}\bigcap_{\bar{\alpha}\leq\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}^{-1}(U_{i}),

and since in any case
f^{-1}_{\alpha}(U_{i})\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
,
the result easily follows.

We now deal with the inclusion from left to right.
Let
\tau
be the topology of
X
.
The proof is by induction on
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
. The base case
\xi=1
is Theorem
5.7
, therefore we can move to the case
\xi>1
.

Assume first that
\xi
is a successor ordinal: in this case, we want to show that
f
is a
\lambda
-limit of
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable measurable functions
f_{\alpha}\colon X\to Y
.
As
f\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
, for every open set
U\subseteq Y
we can write
f^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{i<\lambda}A_{i}
with
A_{i}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
.
Applying Corollary
6.2
to the family
(X\setminus A_{i})_{i<\lambda}
, we obtain a topology
\tau^{\prime}
on
X
such that
(X,\tau^{\prime})\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
,
\tau\subseteq\tau^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(\tau)
, and
A_{i}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{1}(\tau^{\prime})
for every
i<\lambda
.
Then
f
is
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{2}(\tau^{\prime})
-measurable: applying Theorem
5.7
, we get that
f=\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{a}
with each
f_{\alpha}\colon(X,\tau^{\prime})\to Y
continuous. Since
\tau^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(\tau)
, the functions
f_{\alpha}
are
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable as maps between
(X,\tau)
and
Y
, hence we are done.

Now assume that
\xi
is a limit ordinal with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)=\lambda
:
we need to show that
f
is a
\lambda
-limit of a sequence of functions
f_{\alpha}\colon X\to Y
, where each
f_{\alpha}
is
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha}}
-measurable for some
\xi_{\alpha}<\xi
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
Y
is a superclosed subspace of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
(Theorem
2.1
).
Fix a basis
(U_{j})_{j<\lambda}
of
Y
, and let
\{A_{i}\mid i<\lambda\}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
be such that for every
j<\lambda
,
f^{-1}(U_{j})=\bigcup_{i\in K_{j}}(X\setminus A_{i})
for some
K_{j}\subseteq\lambda
.
By Lemma
6.1
, there are a non-decreasing function
\nu\colon\lambda\to\xi
, a subspace
D\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, and a continuous bijection
h\colon D\to X
such that
h^{-1}(A_{i})\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{1}(D)
for every
i<\lambda
, and moreover

h(\boldsymbol{N}_{s}\cap D)\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\nu(\operatorname{lh}(s))}
for every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.52364pt{<\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.9186pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.52364pt{<\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.9186pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.73335pt{<\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.15834pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.91669pt{<\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.34167pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.26932pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
By the choice of the sets
A_{i}
,
the function
f\circ h\colon D\to Y
is thus
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{2}(D)
-measurable.
By Proposition
5.6
, there is a family of functions
(g_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\lambda}
such that
f\circ h=\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}g_{\alpha}
and each
g_{\alpha}\colon D\to Y
is
\lambda
-full with constant
r_{\alpha}
: set
f_{\alpha}=g_{\alpha}\circ h^{-1}
.
Clearly,
f=\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
. Since
g_{\alpha}
is
\lambda
-full with constant
r_{\alpha}
, the range of
g_{\alpha}
consists of (at most)
\lambda
-many points
\{y_{i}\mid i<\lambda\}\subseteq Y
such that for every
i<\lambda

g_{\alpha}^{-1}(y_{i})=\bigcup\{\boldsymbol{N}_{t}\cap D\mid t\in L_{i}\},

for some
L_{i}\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.98872pt{\alpha}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.88469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.98872pt{\alpha}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.88469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.2084pt{\alpha}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-10.1341pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.39174pt{\alpha}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-9.31743pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
. By the choice of
h
, it follows that
f_{\alpha}^{-1}(y_{i})=h(g_{\alpha}^{-1}(y_{i}))\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\nu(\alpha)+1}(X)
. Since
\ran(f_{\alpha})=\ran(g_{\alpha})
has at most
\lambda
-many elements,
f_{\alpha}
is thus
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha}}
-measurable for
\xi_{\alpha}=\nu(\alpha)+1<\xi
.
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
\lambda
be regular,
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and suppose that
Y
is spherically complete. Then the closure under
\lambda
-limits of the collection of all continuous functions is precisely
\mathcal{M}_{<\omega}(X,Y)
.

More generally, for every
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
which is either a successor ordinal, or a limit ordinal of cofinality
\lambda
, the closure of
\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
under
\lambda
-limits is
\mathcal{M}_{<\xi+\omega}(X,Y)
.

Proof.

Exploiting Theorem
6.3
,
an easy induction shows that
\mathcal{M}_{<\xi+\omega}(X,Y)=\bigcup_{n\in\omega}\mathcal{M}_{\xi+n}(X,Y)
is contained in the closure under
\lambda
-limits of
\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
. Since
\operatorname{cof}(\xi+\omega)=\omega<\lambda=\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)
, the reverse inclusion follows from Proposition
4.6
.
∎

Theorem
6.3
tells us that, in order to find an appropriate definition of generalized Baire class functions, we can use
\lambda
-limits at all successor stages and at all limit levels with cofinality
\lambda
.
When
\xi
is a limit ordinal of cofinality smaller than
\lambda
, instead, functions in
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}
can no longer be characterized as
\lambda
-limits of functions in
\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
because of Proposition
4.6
, so a different approach is in order. In this situation, Corollary
4.4
suggests to use limits of the form

\lim\nolimits_{\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}}\circ\lim\nolimits_{\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}.

However, this would be an overdoing: as shown in the next proposition, the inclusion in Corollary
4.4
is in most cases proper, and thus the resulting class would be larger than expected.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\lambda
be regular,
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and let
\xi<\lambda^{+}
be a limit ordinal.
Suppose that
A\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}(X)\setminus\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi+1}(X)
and that there are at least two distinct points
y_{0},y_{1}\in Y
. Let
f\colon X\to Y
be the “characteristic function” of
A
defined by
f(x)=y_{0}
if
x\in A
, and
f(x)=y_{1}
otherwise. Then
f\notin\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
, yet

f\in\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\!\lim\,\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right)
. In particular,

\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)\subsetneq\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right).

Proof.

We first prove a claim which might be of independent interest.

Claim 6.5.1
.

Let
A_{0},\dotsc,A_{n}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
, and let
g\colon X\to Y
be such that
g\restriction(X\setminus\bigcup_{j\leq n}A_{j})
and
g\restriction A_{j}
, for
j\leq n
, are all constant.
Then
g=\lim_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}g_{\alpha}
for some sequence of function
(g_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
with
g_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
.

Proof of the Claim.

Since
g
is constant on each
A_{j}
, we can assume without loss of generality that
A_{j}\cap A_{i}=\emptyset
for every
j<i\leq n
. Let
\bar{y}
be the value of
g
on
X\setminus\bigcup_{j\leq n}A_{j}
, and
y_{j}
be the value of
g
on
A_{j}
, for every
j\leq n
.
Let
(\xi_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
be a strictly increasing sequence of ordinals cofinal in
\xi
.
By Lemma
3.5
(2)
, each
X\setminus A_{j}
can be written as
X\setminus A_{j}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}B^{j}_{\alpha}
where
B^{j}_{\alpha}\subseteq B^{j}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
if
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
and
B^{j}_{\alpha}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha+2}}(X)
.
For every
\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
, let
g_{\alpha}\colon X\to Y
be defined by

g_{\alpha}(x)=\begin{cases}y_{j},&\text{if }x\notin\bigcap_{j\leq n}B^{j}_{\alpha}\text{ and }j\leq n\text{ is smallest such that }x\notin B^{j}_{\alpha},\\
\bar{y},&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Each
g_{\alpha}
is
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha}+2}
-measurable because it is locally constant on a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha}+2}
-partition,
and hence it belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
.
Pick any
x\in X
. If
x\notin\bigcup_{j\leq n}A_{j}
, then for every
j\leq n
there is
\alpha_{j}<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
such that
x\in B^{j}_{\alpha_{j}}
. Set
\bar{\alpha}=\max\{\alpha_{j}\mid j\leq n\}
: then for every
\bar{\alpha}\leq\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
we have
x\in\bigcap_{j\leq n}B^{j}_{\alpha}
, and hence
g_{\alpha}(x)=\bar{y}=g(x)
.
If instead
x\in\bigcup_{j\leq n}A_{j}
, then there is a unique
\bar{\jmath}\leq n
such that
x\in A_{\bar{\jmath}}
because the sets
A_{j}
are assumed to be pairwise disjoint. For each
j<\bar{\jmath}
, let
\alpha_{j}<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
be such that
x\in B^{j}_{\alpha_{j}}
, and set
\bar{\alpha}=\max\{\alpha_{j}\mid j<\bar{\jmath}\}
. Then by construction
g_{\alpha}(x)=y_{j}=g(x)
for every
\bar{\alpha}\leq\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
.
This shows that
g=\lim_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}g_{\alpha}
and concludes our proof.
∎

By Claim
6.5.1
, it suffices to show that
f=\lim_{d\in\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}}f_{d}
, where each
f_{d}\colon X\to Y
is locally constant on both
A_{d}
and
X\setminus A_{d}
for some
A_{d}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
.
To this aim, let
\{A_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\lambda\}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
be such that
A=\bigcup_{\alpha<\lambda}A_{\alpha}
.
For each
d\in\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}
, let
A_{d}=\bigcup\{A_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in d\}
, and then let
f_{d}(x)=y_{0}
if
a\in A_{d}
and
f_{d}(x)=y_{1}
otherwise.
If
x\notin A
, then
f_{d}(x)=y_{1}=f(x)
for all
d\in\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}
. If instead
x\in A
, then
f_{d}(x)=y_{0}=f(x)
for all
d\supseteq\{\alpha\}
, where
\alpha<\lambda
is any ordinal such that
x\in A_{\alpha}
.
This shows that
f=\lim_{d\in\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}}f_{d}
, as desired.
∎

The core problem with using the double limit
\lim_{\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}}\circ\lim_{\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
at limit levels is actually the fact that even when
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
, the class
\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
is not closed under
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
-limits. More in detail, let
\lambda
be regular and
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
. Then for every
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
, the class
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi+1}(X)
is a
\lambda
-algebra. It easily follows that if
D
is a directed set of size smaller than
\lambda
and
f\in D\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
, then
f
is
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi+1}
-measurable. However, this is optimal. To see this, suppose that there is
A\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}(X)\setminus\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
and that
Y
contains at least two distinct points
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
. Let
f\colon X\to Y
be defined by
f(x)=y_{0}
if
x\in A
and
f(x)=y_{1}
otherwise. Then by Claim
6.5.1
the function
f
is a
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
-limit of functions in
\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
, yet it is not
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable by the choice of
A
.

To overcome this difficulty,
we have to perform a deeper analysis of limits over directed sets of small size.
Let
(D,\leq)
be a directed set. A covering
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
of a set
X
is (
D
-
)
increasing
if
X_{d}\subseteq X_{d^{\prime}}
for every
d,d^{\prime}\in D
such that
d\leq d^{\prime}
.

Definition 6.6
.

Let
(D,\leq)
be a directed set, and let

f
and
(f_{d})_{d\in D}
be functions between topological spaces
X
and
Y
. Then
f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
if there exists a
D
-increasing covering
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
of
X
such that
f_{d^{\prime}}\restriction X_{d}=f\restriction X_{d}
for every
d,d^{\prime}\in D
with
d^{\prime}\geq d
.

Notice that if
f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
, then there is a
canonical
increasing covering
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
of
X
witnessing that, namely,

(6.1)

X_{d}=\{x\in X\mid f_{d^{\prime}}(x)=f(x)\text{ for all }d^{\prime}\geq d\}.

Obviously,
f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
implies
f=\lim_{d\in D}f_{d}
. The reverse is not true in general. For example, assume that
\lambda
is regular and consider
D=\lambda
, ordered as usual. Let
f\colon\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
be constant with value
0^{(\lambda)}
and, for every
\alpha<\lambda
, let
f_{\alpha}\colon\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
be constant with value
0^{(\alpha)}\conc 1^{(\lambda)}
. Then
f=\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
, but
f\neq\widehat{\lim}_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
for the simple reason that
f(x)\neq f_{\alpha}(x)
for every
\alpha<\lambda
and
x\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

In contrast, we are now going to show that if
D
has small size, then the two notions of limit coincide. Let
\mathcal{D}_{<\lambda}
be the collection of all directed sets of size strictly smaller than
\lambda
. Clearly,
\mathcal{D}_{<\lambda}
is a proper subclass of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\lambda
be regular,
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and let
D\in\mathcal{D}_{<\lambda}
.
Let
(f_{d})_{d\in D}
be a family of functions from
X
to
Y
.
Then for every
f\colon X\to Y
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

f=\lim_{d\in D}f_{d}
;

(2)

for every
x\in X
there exists
d\in D
such that
f_{d^{\prime}}(x)=f(x)
for every
d^{\prime}\geq d
;

(3)

f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.1
, without loss of generality we can assume that
X,Y\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

(1)

\Rightarrow

(2)

Fix
x\in X
. For every
\beta<\lambda
there exists
d_{\beta}\in D
such that
f_{d}(x)\restriction\beta=f(x)\restriction\beta
, for all
d\geq d_{\beta}
.
For every
d\in D
, let
G_{d}=\{\beta<\lambda\mid d_{\beta}=d\}
. Since
(G_{d})_{d\in D}
is a covering of
\lambda
in
|D|
-many pieces and
\lambda
is regular, there is
\bar{d}\in D
such that
|G_{\bar{d}}|=\lambda
, so that
G_{\bar{d}}
is unbounded in
\lambda
.
It follows that for every
d^{\prime}\geq\bar{d}
there are arbitrarily large
\beta<\lambda
such that
f_{d^{\prime}}(x)\restriction\beta=f(x)\restriction\beta
, and thus
f_{d^{\prime}}(x)=f(x)
.

(2)

\Rightarrow

(3)

For
d\in D
, let
X_{d}
be as in equation (
6.1
).
By definition
X_{d}\subseteq X_{d^{\prime}}
whenever
d\leq d^{\prime}
, and
(2)
ensures that
\bigcup_{d\in D}X_{d}=X
.
It is then easy to verify that
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
witnesses
f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
.

(3)

\Rightarrow

(1)

Obvious.
∎

Although they are equivalent when
D\in\mathcal{D}_{<\lambda}
, the advantage of using the limit operator
\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}
instead of
\lim_{d\in D}
is that we can naturally impose a definability condition on the increasing coverings involved in its definition. Lemma
6.10
and Theorem
6.11
show that this move fixes the problems encountered when dealing with limit levels of cofinality smaller than
\lambda
.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
(D,\leq)
be a directed set,
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
be an ordinal, and let

f
and
(f_{d})_{d\in D}
be functions between topological spaces
X
and
Y
. Then
f=\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}_{d\in D}f_{d}
if there exists a
D
-increasing covering
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
of
X
witnessing
f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
such that

X_{d}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
for every
d\in D
.

Remark 6.9
.

It can be shown that if
f=\widehat{\lim}_{d\in D}f_{d}
and
f_{d}\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
for every
d\in D
, then the canonical increasing covering
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
from equation (
6.1
) is such that
X_{d}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
for all
d\in D
, because
X_{d}=\bigcap_{d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime}\geq d}\{x\in X\mid f_{d^{\prime}}(x)=f_{d^{\prime\prime}}(x)\}
. Thus in Definition
6.8
we are adopting the weakest possible definability requirement.

In contrast to the other results of this section, which all require
\lambda
to be regular, the next two results hold unconditionally, and indeed they will be used in Section
6.2
too.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
. For every
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
, if
f\in D\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
, then
f\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
.

Proof.

Let
f=\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}_{d\in D}f_{d}
, as witnessed by the
D
-increasing covering
(X_{d})_{d\in D}
. Then for every open set
U\subseteq Y
we have

f^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{d\in D}(f_{d}^{-1}(U)\cap X_{d}),

so that
f^{-1}(U)\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
because
X_{d}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
.
∎

The proof of the next result is essentially the same of Corollary
4.4
: we only have to check that, thanks to Lemma
6.10
, the definability condition added in Definition
6.8
allows us to obtain an equality instead of an inclusion.

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and further assume that
\dim(X)=\dim(Y)=0
if
\mu=\omega
.
For every limit ordinal
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
,

\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume
X,Y\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.80667pt{\mu}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.30214pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.80667pt{\mu}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.30214pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.07837pt{\mu}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.71799pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.2617pt{\mu}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.90132pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
by Theorems
2.1
and
2.3
.
The inclusion from left to right follows again from Proposition
4.3
and Lemma
4.1
, together with Remark
4.2
.

For the reverse inclusion, observe that
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
by Lemma
6.10
, so it is enough to check that
\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
.
Since
|\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}|=\lambda
this can easily be obtain from the same argument used in the implication
(3)

\Rightarrow

(1)
of Theorem
4.5
, the only difference being that now the functions
f_{d}
are assumed to be
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable.
∎

We are now ready to discuss some simple and natural generalizations to the uncountable setting of the Baire stratification of Borel functions, with the goal of obtaining an analogue of Theorem
1.2
.
Recall that in the classical case
\lambda=\omega
, the class of Baire class
\xi
functions is defined by taking continuous functions if
\xi=0
, and (
\omega
-)limits of functions of lower Baire class if
\xi>0
.
As discussed at length, when moving to a regular cardinal we can naturally use
\lambda
-limits at successor stages and at limit levels with cofinality
\lambda
, while in the remaining cases we have to use a different limit operator. This leads us to the following first proposal.
To simplify the notation, we write
\mathcal{B}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
instead of
\bigcup_{\xi^{\prime}<\xi}\mathcal{B}_{\xi^{\prime}}(X,Y)
.

Definition 6.12
.

Let
\lambda
be regular, and let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
. For every
\xi<\lambda^{+}
, we recursively define the collection
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)
of
\lambda
-Baire class
\xi
functions
as follows:

(1)

\mathcal{B}_{0}(X,Y)
is the collection of all continuous functions
f\colon X\to Y
.

(2)

If
\xi
is either a successor ordinal or a limit ordinal with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)=\lambda
, then

\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\lambda\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{B}_{<\xi}(X,Y).

(3)

If
\xi
is a limit ordinal with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
, then

\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{B}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right).

Definition
6.12
is designed to be as close as possible to the classical definition and to minimize the kind of limit operators involved, at the price of having to consider different cases depending on the ordinal
\xi<\lambda^{+}
.
In the opposite direction, one could desire to have a uniform definition which maximizes the class of limit operators that can be employed. This leads us to the idea of allowing the use of the special double-limits from part
(3)
of Definition
6.12
at every level of the hierarchy, and to replace
\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}
and
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
with any pair of directed sets in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
We again write
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
instead of
\bigcup_{\xi^{\prime}<\xi}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi^{\prime}}(X,Y)
.

Definition 6.13
.

Let
\lambda
be regular, and let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
. For every
\xi<\lambda^{+}
, we recursively define the classes of functions
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)
as follows:

(1)

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(X,Y)
is the collection of all continuous functions
f\colon X\to Y
.

(2)

If
\xi>0
, then

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\left(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right).

We are now going to show that for most spaces
X
and
Y
, both definitions do the job, and they are in fact equivalent to each other.
We remark that the two options are the extremes of a whole range of intermediate possibilities, which however would give rise to the very same classes of functions because of the next theorem.

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
\lambda
be regular,
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and suppose that
Y
is spherically complete.
Then for every
\xi<\lambda^{+}
,

\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y).

Proof.

By induction on
\xi<\lambda^{+}
. The equality
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
follows from Theorems
6.3
and
6.11
, while the inclusion
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)
is obvious. Finally, to prove

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
it is enough to use Lemma
6.10
and the usual computation yielding
D\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
for every
D\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
∎

The assumption that
Y
be spherically complete in Theorem
6.14
is very mild, as every
\lambda
-Cauchy-complete
6
6

6

These spaces are one of the natural generalizations to the uncountable setting of Polish spaces, and they are indeed called
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces in the specialized literature (see e.g.
[
AMRS23
]
).

X\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
can easily be turned into a spherically complete space by adding at most
\lambda
-many points.
However, if one wants to dispense from such assumption and work with arbitrary spaces in
\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, it is enough to modify Definition
6.12
as follows:

•

when
\xi
is a successor ordinal,
\lambda
-limits have to be replaced with
\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}
-limits;

•

at all limit levels
\xi
we use the double limit
\lim_{\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}}\circ\ \widehat{\lim}^{\xi}_{\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
, independently of the cofinality of
\xi
.

This works because even after such modification, in the first part of the proof of Theorem
6.14
we can still prove the inclusion
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)
by using Proposition
4.3
and (a suitable variant of) Corollary
4.4
.
This alternative approach turns out to work also in the singular case, as briefly discussed in the next section.

6.2.
The singular case

We now move to the case where
\mu=\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)<\lambda
. There are two major differences from the regular case:

(1)

First,
\lambda
-limits are no longer relevant, as
\lambda
-limits and
\mu
-limits are obviously equivalent.

(2)

Secondly, when
\xi
is limit we do not have to distinguish cases depending on the cofinality of
\xi
: since
\lambda
is singular,
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
for every limit
\xi<\lambda^{+}
.

This naturally leads us to the following variation of Definition
6.12
.

Definition 6.15
.

Let
\lambda
be singular, and let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
. For every
\xi<\lambda^{+}
, we recursively define the collection
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)
of
\lambda
-Baire class
\xi
functions
as follows:

(1)

\mathcal{B}_{0}(X,Y)
is the collection of all continuous functions
f\colon X\to Y
.

(2)

If
\xi
is a successor ordinal, then

\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{B}_{<\xi}(X,Y).

(3)

If
\xi
is a limit ordinal, then

\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{B}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right).

Moreover, it still makes sense to consider the alternative approach taken in Definition
6.13
: in this case, the definition of the classes
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)
needs not to be updated. As in the regular case, it turns out that both definitions allow us to generalize Theorem
1.2
.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
\lambda
be singular,
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and further assume that
\dim(X)=\dim(Y)=0
if
\mu=\omega
.
Then for every
\xi<\lambda^{+}
,

\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y).

Proof.

We argue as in the proof of Theorem
6.14
, with the following exception. Instead of showing that
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
, we just prove the inclusion
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)
using Proposition
4.3
(in the successor case) and a suitable variant of Corollary
4.4
(in the limit case) instead of Theorems
6.3
and
6.11
. The rest of the proof goes unchanged.
∎

7.
Uniform limits

This short section aims at generalizing the results from
[
MR09
, Section 3]
to the uncountable setting. The theme is that of characterizing
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable functions in terms of
uniform
limits of simpler functions. Although somewhat unrelated to the previous sections, these results add more information on the structure of
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable functions with respect to another kind of limit, and thus we feel that they fit well with the general topic of the paper.

Recall that in Section
2.2
we fixed a field
\mathbb{G}
with degree
\mu=\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)
and a strictly decreasing sequence
(r_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\mu}
coinitial in
\mathbb{G}^{+}
.
By the choice we made,
r_{\alpha+1}+r_{\alpha+1}\leq r_{\alpha}
for every
\alpha<\mu
.
Let
\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{\lambda}
be the collection of all
\mathbb{G}
-metric spaces of weight at most
\lambda
.
Given a directed set
D
, a topological space
X\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and a
\mathbb{G}
-metric space
(Y,d_{Y})\in\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{\lambda}
, we say that a function
f\colon X\to Y
is the
uniform
D
-limit
of a family
(f_{d})_{d\in D}
of functions from
X
to
Y
, and we write
f=\ulim_{d\in D}f_{d}
, if for every
\epsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
there is
d\in D
such that
d_{Y}(f_{d^{\prime}}(x),f(x))<\epsilon
for every
x\in X
and
d^{\prime}\geq d
. It is not hard to see that one could restrict the attention to directed sets of size at most
\mu
: if
f=\ulim_{d\in D}f_{d}
, then there is
D^{\prime}\subseteq D
with
|D^{\prime}|=\min\{\mu,|D|\}
such that
f=\ulim_{d\in D^{\prime}}f_{d}
.

As in the classical setting, it is easy to check that continuous functions are closed under uniform
\lambda
-limits.
We generalize this to higher levels and to all directed sets
D
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
D
be any directed set,
X\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
,
(Y,d_{Y})\in\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{\lambda}
, and
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
is closed under uniform
D
-limits.

Proof.

Suppose that
f=\ulim_{d\in D}f_{d}
with
f_{d}\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
for every
d\in D
.
We need to show that given any
y\in Y
and
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
, the preimage of the open ball
B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon)
belongs to
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
. Let
\beta<\mu
be such that
r_{\beta}<\varepsilon
, and for each
\alpha<\mu
set
\varepsilon_{\alpha}=\varepsilon-r_{\beta+\alpha}
. Then
0<\varepsilon_{\alpha}<\varepsilon
, and for every
\varepsilon^{\prime}<\varepsilon
there is
\alpha<\mu
such that
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\alpha}
.

By choice of
(f_{d})_{d\in D}
, for every
\alpha<\mu
there is
d_{\alpha}\in D
such that
d_{Y}(f_{d}(x),f(x))<r_{\beta+\alpha}
for every
x\in X
and
d\geq d_{\alpha}
. We claim that

f^{-1}(B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon))=\bigcup_{\alpha<\mu}f^{-1}_{d_{\alpha}}(B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon_{\alpha})),

from which the result clearly follows.

For the inclusion from right to left, fix
\alpha<\mu
and a point
x\in f^{-1}_{d_{\alpha}}(B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon_{\alpha}))
. By the triangle inequality and the choice of
d_{\alpha}
,

d_{Y}(y,f(x))\leq d_{Y}(y,f_{d_{\alpha}}(x))+d_{Y}(f_{d_{\alpha}}(x),f(x))<\varepsilon_{\alpha}+r_{\beta+\alpha}=\varepsilon,

so that
x\in f^{-1}(B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon))
.

Conversely, given
x\in f^{-1}(B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon))
let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=d_{Y}(y,f(x))<\varepsilon
. Let
\alpha<\mu
be such that
r_{\beta+\alpha}<\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\prime}
, so that
\varepsilon^{\prime}+r_{\beta+\alpha+1}+r_{\beta+\alpha+1}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}+r_{\beta+\alpha}\leq\varepsilon
and hence
\varepsilon^{\prime}+r_{\beta+\alpha+1}\leq\varepsilon-r_{\beta+\alpha+1}=\varepsilon_{a+1}
. Then by the triangular inequality and the choice of
d_{\alpha}

d_{Y}(y,f_{d_{\alpha+1}}(x))\leq d_{Y}(y,f(x))+d_{Y}(f(x),f_{d_{\alpha+1}}(x))<\varepsilon^{\prime}+r_{\beta+\alpha+1}\leq\varepsilon_{\alpha+1}.

Therefore
x\in f^{-1}_{d_{\alpha+1}}(B_{d_{Y}}(y,\varepsilon_{\alpha+1}))
and we are done.
∎

Let
X
and
Y
be topological spaces, and let
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
. A
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi}
-function
is a map
f\colon X\to Y
such that
f^{-1}(A)\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
, for every
A\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(Y)
. The set of
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi}
-functions is denoted by
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)
.
It is easy to check that
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi}
-functions provide an alternative stratification of
\lambda^{+}
-Borel measurable functions: indeed,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi\cdot\omega}(X,Y)
for every
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
.
Moreover, under mild assumptions on the spaces
X
and
Y
the set
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)
is a proper subclass of
\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
.
Notice also that if a function is locally in
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)
on a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-partition of
X
of size at most
\lambda
, then it is a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi}
-function itself.
We now have all the tools to mimic the proof of
[
MR09
, Theorem 3.5]
and get the following characterization of
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
in terms of uniform
\lambda
-limits of simpler functions.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
,
(Y,d_{Y})\in\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{\lambda}
, and
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
. For every function
f\colon X\to Y
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

f\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
;

(2)

f=\ulim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
, where each
f_{\alpha}
is locally constant on a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-partition of
X
of size at most
\lambda
;

(3)

f=\ulim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
with
f_{\alpha}\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)
for all
\alpha<\lambda
.

Moreover, in part
(2)
we can replace locally constant functions with locally Lipschitz or even just locally continuous maps.

Proof.

The implication
(2)

\Rightarrow

(3)
follows from the fact that every constant (or Lipshitz, or continuous) function belongs to
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)
. Moreover, if
(3)
holds then
f_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
for all
\alpha<\lambda
, and thus
f\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
by Lemma
7.1
, so that
(1)
is satisfied. It remains to prove
(1)

\Rightarrow

(2)
.

Let
\{y_{i}\mid i<\lambda\}
be a dense subset of
Y
, and for each
\alpha<\mu
and
i<\lambda
let
U^{i}_{\alpha}=B_{d_{Y}}(y_{i},r_{\alpha})
. Fix
\alpha<\mu
. The family
\{U^{i}_{\alpha}\mid i<\lambda\}
is an open covering of
Y
.
Since
f\in\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
, each set
A^{i}_{\alpha}=f^{-1}(U^{i}_{\alpha})
belongs to
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}(X)
.
By the
\lambda
-reduction property (Theorem
3.6
), we can find a disjoint
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-covering
\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}=\{B^{i}_{\alpha}\mid i<\lambda\}
of
X
such that
B^{i}_{\alpha}\subseteq A^{i}_{\alpha}
for every
i<\lambda
. Thus the nonempty elements of
\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}
form a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-partition of
X
. Let
f_{\alpha}
be defined on an arbitrary
x\in X
by letting
f(x)=y_{i}
, where
i<\lambda
is such that
x\in B^{i}_{\alpha}
.
Then
f_{\alpha}
is locally constant on the above mentioned
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-partition, and
f=\ulim_{\alpha<\lambda}f_{\alpha}
by the choice of the open sets
U^{i}_{\alpha}
.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
,
(Y,d_{Y})\in\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{\lambda}
, and
1\leq\xi<\lambda^{+}
. Then

\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)=\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\ulim\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y).

Proof.

The left-to-right inclusion follows from Theorem
7.2
, while the reverse inclusion holds by
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\xi}(X,Y)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\xi}(X,Y)
and Lemma
7.1
.
∎

By virtue of Theorems
6.14
and
6.16
, all the results of this section also hold for the collection
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)
of
\lambda
-Baire class
\xi
functions, for every
\xi<\lambda^{+}
(under the appropriate hypotheses on
X
and
Y
).
In particular, while
\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(X,Y)
is far from being closed under
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
-limits, which indeed generate the next class
\mathcal{B}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
, it is instead closed under
uniform

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
-limits.

8.
Final remarks and open questions

Theorem
4.5
, Theorem
6.14
, and Theorem
6.16
provide a quite satisfactory answer to our initial problem. However, as discussed at the end of Section
4
, it would be interesting to understand if it is possible to further reduce the number of limits needed to perform this kind of analysis. In particular, in the regular case it is natural to wonder whether sequential limits (of any length) suffice.
An attempt in this direction was made in
[
Nob18
]
, where
\lambda
-Baire class
\xi
functions are defined as the collection of all
\kappa
-limits, for
\kappa\leq\lambda
, of functions with
\lambda
-Baire class smaller than
\xi
.
The setup is that of a regular cardinal
\lambda
and of functions between some fixed subset
X
of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
and the whole generalized Baire space
Y=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.71252pt{\lambda}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.00087pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.01112pt{\lambda}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.50279pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.19446pt{\lambda}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.68613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, thus this definition is precisely what we are considering in the present discussion.
After providing a game characterization of the classes
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
, in
[
Nob18
, Theorem 4.12]
it is claimed that all
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}
-measurable functions are of
\lambda
-Baire class
\xi
according to the previous definition.
The proof is by induction on
\xi<\lambda^{+}
, but as observed it has to fail at the first limit level with small cofinality, i.e. at stage
\xi=\omega
: since the functions of finite
\lambda
-Baire class are those in
\mathcal{M}_{<\omega}(X,Y)
, any
\kappa
-limit of such functions must be in
\mathcal{M}_{<\omega}(X,Y)
itself if
\kappa=\lambda
(Proposition
4.6
), or it has to be
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi+1}
-measurable if
\kappa<\lambda
(because
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi+1}
is a
\lambda
-algebra).
In other words: when confining ourselves to sequential limits, in order to capture
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}
-measurable functions for
\xi
limit with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
it is necessary to use (at least) double limits.
Despite the failure of the naïve approach undertaken in
[
Nob18
]
