, using nested sequential limits it might still be possible to answer affirmatively the following questions, which to the best of our knowledge are still open.

Question 8.1
.

Let
\lambda
be regular, and
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
. Does the collection of all
\lambda^{+}
-Borel measurable functions from
X
to
Y
coincide with the closure of continuous functions under
\kappa
-limits, where
\kappa
varies among regular cardinals up to
\lambda
? If
\xi<\lambda^{+}
is limit with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
, is it true that
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)=\lambda\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right)
? Can we at least have
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)\subseteq\lambda\text{-}\!\lim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right)
?

A positive answer to these questions would provide optimal results.
As a partial contribution in this direction, we notice the following.

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and let
\xi<\lambda^{+}
be a limit ordinal with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
.

(1)

If
f\colon X\to Y
is locally constant on a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-partition of
X
of size at most
\lambda
, then
f\in\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
.

(2)

If
f\colon X\to Y
is
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-measurable, then
f\in\lambda\text{-}\!\ulim\left(\operatorname{cof}(\xi)\text{-}\widehat{\lim}{}^{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)\right)
.

Proof.

(1)

The proof is similar to that of Claim
6.5.1
. Let
(A_{j})_{j<J}
, for some
J\leq\lambda
, be a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi}
-partition of
X
such that
f
is locally constant on it. By Lemma
3.5
(if
\mu>\omega
) and
[
DMR
, Proposition 4.2.1]
(if
\mu=\omega
), there are a strictly increasing sequence
(\xi_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
cofinal in
\xi
and sets
B^{j}_{\alpha}\in\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha}}(X)
such that
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{j}_{\alpha}\subseteq B^{j}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
A_{j}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}B^{j}_{\alpha}
, for every
j<J
. Fix any
\bar{y}\in Y
and define
g_{\alpha}\colon X\to Y
by setting

g_{\alpha}(x)=\begin{cases}f(x)&\text{if }x\in\bigcup_{j<J}B^{j}_{\alpha}\\
\bar{y}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then
g_{\alpha}
is
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi_{\alpha}+1}
-measurable, and
f=\widehat{\lim}^{\xi}_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}g_{a}
, as witnessed by the increasing covering
(X_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
of
X
given by
X_{\alpha}=\bigcup_{j<J}B^{j}_{\alpha}
.

(2)

Use part
(1)
and Theorem
7.2
.
∎

We also observe that by Theorem
7.2
, to deal with the case of arbitrary functions in
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)
for
\xi<\lambda^{+}
limit with
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)<\lambda
, it is enough to solve the problem for functions which are locally constant on a
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}
-partition of size at most
\lambda
. Arguing as in Section
6
(and using in particular Claim
6.5.1
), such functions can be obtained through a limit operator of the form
\lim_{\lambda}\circ\lim_{\operatorname{cof}(\xi)}
if the
\lambda^{+}\text{-}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{0}_{\xi+1}
-partition is finite or, more generally, of size smaller than
\operatorname{cof}(\xi)
. Unfortunately, although the remaining gap might seem small, we cannot yet close it.

In the case of a singular cardinal
\lambda
, instead, it seems hard to conjecture that sequential limits can suffice, as only short limits are available because
\lambda
-limits are equivalent to
\mu
-limits, where as usual
\mu=\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)<\lambda
. However, it makes sense to ask whether
\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}
-limits are enough to describe the whole structure of
\lambda^{+}
-Borel measurable functions and its stratification given by
\lambda
-Baire class
\xi
functions. The question makes sense also in the regular case.

Question 8.3
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
.
Does the closure under
\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}
-limits of
\mathcal{M}_{1}(X,Y)
coincide with the collection of all
\lambda^{+}
-Borel measurable functions?
In particular, is it true that
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Fin}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
when
\xi<\lambda^{+}
is a limit ordinal?

Finally, recall that in Theorem
4.8
we proved that there is a single directed set in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, namely
\widehat{\mathrm{Fin}}_{\lambda}
, which can generate the whole class of
\lambda^{+}
-Borel measurable functions, globally. This naturally raises the question of whether it can also give a level-by-level result. As usual, the answer is positive for successor levels and, if
\lambda
is regular, also for limit levels of cofinality
\lambda
. However, the remaining cases are still unclear.

Question 8.4
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathscr{M}_{\lambda}
, and let
\xi<\lambda^{+}
be a limit ordinal.
Is it true that
\mathcal{M}_{\xi+1}(X,Y)=\widehat{\mathrm{Fin}}_{\lambda}\text{-}\!\lim\mathcal{M}_{<\xi}(X,Y)
?
